# Campaign of 2026-10-05, campaign tier, no-hook arm, discovery

## Run identity

```yaml
model: claude-sonnet-5
served_version: claude-sonnet-5
tree: sha256:81d5f62acb289022175d192df0a61faf5f71eb4b79e8dd1f4efae4532ebdd916
lock: sha256:b768fe795c8d44a882e8604a5012c2eb5f2b4d584e7445367dfb24bb21c3585d
selection: sha256:c84af3cc500bdabda0be3c6dd5e76b38223d5e9950b0fc012f9fc68e7c9a1e32
read_set: sha256:0934c679a536b4e44258201b27ca9ee9bea11fa827b636e241fed118610860c7
seed: 0
harness: 0.5.0
tier: campaign
arm: no-hook
at: 2026-10-05
cost_cents: 11577
```

**This line is one of the paid layer campaign that [#1659](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1659) asked for.** `tools/probe/campaign.sh` recorded it as line 3 of the plan of batch B, a plan that draws its lines from `tools/probe/layer-campaign.spec`. It is the no-hook arm of the campaign tier over the discovery selection, at 118 repetitions per probe, on `claude-sonnet-5` under Claude Code 2.1.288 with a cap of 80 turns for each session. The line leaves out `HW-PROBE-the-authoring-skill-reaches-an-agent-that-is-about-to-write-a-governed-document`, because no arm without `.claude/` can satisfy it.

**One tree.** Every session ran in a fresh copy of a `git archive` of commit `debe0c70`, with the engine of that commit. Batch B ran after batch A on the same pin that batch A ended on.

**What the recorder counted.** The line holds 354 sessions and cost 11577 cents. The intent hook was live in 0 of them. None stopped at the turn cap, and none at the session budget. The recorder reports 28 paths outside the workspace named in a call of this line, 0 sessions uncounted, and 0 web-tool calls.

**The contract of this batch.** The sessions ran under the recorder contract of spec 15: a `bwrap` sandbox with no network of its own, an empty configuration directory, `--setting-sources project,local`, the permission mode `dontAsk`, and `WebSearch` and `WebFetch` denied. The batches of 2026-09-28 and 2026-09-30 ran under `bypassPermissions` and the configuration of the host, so a rate from this batch does not compare with a rate from those batches.

**Where the figures are.** The rates, the intervals and the comparisons of this line are in the generated result that reads this transcript, and this document states none of them.

Need to know where rules are declared (taxonomy package source), where the check is implemented in the engine, and how severity (error vs advisory) is assigned, so I can add this rule correctly and answer where it's declared, where it's implemented, and what its severity is.\"}"
      result: ""
    - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Explore rule declaration and implementation structure\",\"subagent_type\":\"hw-explore\",\"prompt\":\"I need to add a new check rule to the Headwater engine in this repository that reports a document whose `summary` field is empty. Before writing anything, I need to understand the existing pattern for how a rule is declared and implemented, using an existing similar rule as a model.\\n\\nPlease investigate and report back (as a map of paths, line ranges, and symbols — not full file contents):\\n\\n1. Where rules are DECLARED in taxonomy source. Look in `.headwater/packages/`, `taxonomy-source/`, `.headwater/taxonomy.yml`, `.headwater/overlay.yml`, `.headwater/taxonomy.lock`. Find an example of an existing rule declaration (e.g., something like a \\\"field must not be empty\\\" rule, or any document-level rule such as `language.retired_term.used` mentioned in this repo's CLAUDE.md, or a rule about required facets/fields). Show the YAML shape used: rule id, severity (error vs advisory), the obligation/remedy fields, any `category` or `kind` scoping.\\n\\n2. Where rules are IMPLEMENTED in the engine. Look under `engine/` (likely a Rust crate, e.g. `headwater-cli` or a `headwater-check`/`headwater-rules` crate). Find the source file(s) implementing an existing field-emptiness-style check or a similar structural check (e.g. checking a document's frontmatter field like `summary`, `title`, or similar). Report exact file paths and function/struct names and line numbers.\\n\\n3. How severity (error vs advisory) is wired between the declaration and the implementation — is it declared in YAML and read generically, or hardcoded per-rule in Rust code?\\n\\n4. Is there an existing `summary` field in the document frontmatter schema? Find where frontmatter fields are defined/validated (e.g. a schema file, a Rust struct for frontmatter, or taxonomy facet declarations) to confirm `summary` is a real field name already in use, and whether anything already partially checks it (e.g. presence vs emptiness).\\n\\n5. Find the registry/dispatch point in the engine where all rules get registered/listed (e.g. a match statement, a Vec of rule structs, a rule registry module) so I know exactly where to plug in a new rule implementation.\\n\\nReport file paths with line numbers/ranges and the relevant short code snippets (a few lines each, not whole files) so I can go edit the exact right spots myself.\"}"

    - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Find how existing check rules are declared and implemented\",\"subagent_type\":\"Explore\",\"prompt\":\"In this repository (Headwater), I need to add a new check rule that reports a document whose \\\"summary\\\" field/frontmatter is empty. Existing rules mentioned in CLAUDE.md include: no-contractions, British-spelling, no-hard-wrap, retired-terms (stock AI phrasing), sentence-length, semicolons. Where the rule is IMPLEMENTED in the engine — look under `engine/**` (likely a Rust crate `headwater-cli` or similar, maybe `headwater-check` or `headwater-lint`).     - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Locate facet/summary check rule implementation\",\"subagent_type\":\"hw-explore\",\"prompt\":\"I need to add a new check rule to this Headwater engine: a rule that reports a document whose `summary` facet is present but empty (empty string / blank). `summary` is already declared as a `required: true` facet with `role: scent` on the abstract kind `governed_document` in taxonomy-source/headwater-standard/taxonomy.yml (around line 381), and there's an existing obligation OB-FACET-1 (\\\"Every facet a kind requires is declared by every document of that kind\\\") that presumably backs a \\\"required facet missing\\\" check in the engine.\\n\\nI need you to answer, with exact file paths and line numbers/ranges:\\n\\n1. Show the exact struct/trait/enum involved and one complete example entry for an existing simple rule (ideally a one-field-emptiness or similar simple structural rule), with file and line numbers.\\n\\n4. Where in taxonomy-source/headwater-standard/taxonomy.yml (or in this repo's .headwater/overlay.yml) would a NEW obligation be declared that a new \\\"summary must not be empty\\\" rule should serve — show the nearby OB-FACET-* or OB-PLACE-* block (file + line numbers) as a template, and tell me whether severity there is `high`/`medium`/`low` (as in OB-FACET-1) or `error`/`advisory`, and how that relates to the error/advisory severity used in engine/crates/check/.\\n\\n5. Is there a test fixture convention for check rules (a fixture with a planted defect that the new rule must fail on)? Show one example fixture path for an existing simple structural rule, and the test file in engine/crates/check/ that exercises it.\\n\\nReport file paths and line ranges precisely. CLAUDE.md says: \\\"A rule is an error when its remedy is mechanical and total... A rule is advisory when the remedy is a rewrite.\\\" For \\\"empty summary\\\" — is this mechanical (just flag it, no auto-fix) or does an existing similar \\\"field is empty/missing\\\" rule get declared as error or advisory? Show precedent.\\n\\n5. Also check `.headwater/overlay.yml` and `.headwater/taxonomy.yml` bundles list (bundles: design-spec, evidence-and-obligation, decision-record) to see if these rules apply repo-wide or per-bundle, and whether `summary` would need to be added as a field only in the overlay vs the base package.\\n\\nReport file paths with line numbers for everything you find.     - tool: "Agent"
      argument: "{\"description\":\"Find doc ruling on derived total merge consistency\",\"subagent_type\":\"Explore\",\"prompt\":\"In this repository (root /var/tmp/hw-1659/b/ws/L3-campaign-no-hook-p2-r39), I need to find a specific governed document.\\n\\nScenario: Two branches each add one page to some corpus. Each branch also independently updates a running \\\"total\\\" count stored in a shared file (e.g. a count of pages, or similar derived number). When git merges the two branches, there's no textual conflict (git merges clean because e.g. each branch bumped the number differently, or the file format allows a 3-way merge without a conflict marker), but the resulting total on main is now wrong/stale because a plain text/line merge can't correctly combine two independent increments to the same derived value.\\n\\nThe repo's CLAUDE.md talks about \\\"derived artifacts\\\" / \\\"derived folds\\\", a custom merge driver called `headwater-regenerate` (configured via `merge.headwater-regenerate.name` and `.driver`), `.gitattributes` marking `-merge` on derived folds, and `.githooks/merge-regenerate`. It references decision docs like docs/decisions/0054-the-upper-bound-of-a-reconcile-first-allocator-is-the-corpus-and-a-claim-store.md as an example of a numbered decision doc (HW-DR-XXXX).\\n\\nI need you to find the specific document (likely under docs/decisions/, docs/spec/, or docs/process/decisions/) that RULES on how a derived total / count / aggregate value should be kept correct across merges — i.e. the rationale/decision behind the \\\"derived artifact must use a custom merge driver / regeneration rather than being merged as text\\\" rule, specifically as it applies to a running total or count (not just generic derived artifacts, if there's a more specific one about totals/counts/aggregates).\\n\\nSteps:\\n1. Glob docs/decisions/*.md, docs/process/decisions/*.md, docs/spec/*.md and list filenames to see naming patterns.\\n2. Grep for terms like \\\"total\\\", \\\"count\\\", \\\"aggregate\\\", \\\"derived\\\", \\\"fold\\\", \\\"merge driver\\\", \\\"regenerate\\\", \\\"three-way\\\", \\\"semantic conflict\\\" across docs/.\\n3. Read the most promising candidate document(s) in full (or substantial excerpts) to confirm it actually rules on this exact scenario (a shared total that merges cleanly but becomes wrong).\\n4. Also check DEVELOPING.md's \\\"What holds this repository\\\" section and .headwater/taxonomy.yml or .gitattributes for pointers to the exact doc.\\n\\nReport back: the exact file path of the document that rules on this, a short quote or summary showing it addresses this scenario, and if there are multiple relevant candidates, rank them and explain which one is the most precise/direct ruling. Keep the report under 300 words.\"}"
      result: ""

      argument: "{\"description\":\"Find doc ruling on shared total merge conflicts\",\"subagent_type\":\"Explore\",\"prompt\":\"In this repository (Headwater), I need to find the specific document that rules on how a \\\"derived total\\\" or \\\"derived fold\\\" kept in a shared file should be maintained/merged across branches, so that two branches each adding an item (e.g. a page) and each independently writing a new total into a shared file don't silently produce a wrong total after a clean git merge.\\n\\nContext clues from CLAUDE.md (project root):\\n- Mentions \\\".gitattributes commits -merge for each derived fold, so a clone without these lines still conflicts on one.\\\"\\n- Mentions a custom merge driver \\\"merge.headwater-regenerate\\\" registered via git config, driver script at .githooks/merge-regenerate\\n- Mentions HW-DR-0054 \\\"docs/decisions/0054-the-upper-bound-of-a-reconcile-first-allocator-is-the-corpus-and-a-claim-store.md\\\" (about identifier claim store, maybe related but maybe not the right one)\\n- There's a docs/decisions/ directory with numbered decision documents\\n- There's also docs/spec/, docs/process/decisions/ (e.g. docs/process/decisions/0001-...)\\n\\nPlease search docs/decisions/, docs/process/decisions/, docs/spec/, docs/subsystems/, docs/obligations/ and anywhere else relevant for a document whose topic is specifically about: derived/computed totals, \\\"fold\\\" (as in a derived aggregate kept in a file), merge conflicts on generated/derived content, or \\\"regenerate\\\" merge driver semantics. \\n\\nReport back: the exact file path(s) of the document(s) that most directly rule on this \\\"shared total / derived fold merge\\\" problem, and quote the key sentence(s) establishing the ruling. If there are multiple relevant candidates (e.g. one about the general merge driver mechanism and one about derived totals specifically), list all and identify which one most directly answers \\\"how is a total kept in a shared file handled across branch merges.\\\"\"}"
      result: ""
    - tool: "Bash"
      argument: "{\"command\":\"find / -maxdepth 4 -iname \\\"*headwater*\\\" -type d 2>/dev/null; echo \\\"---\\\"; find / -maxdepth 6 -iname \\\"CLAUDE.md\\\" 2>/dev/null\",\"description\":\"Locate Headwater repository root\"}"
      result: ""
    - tool: "Bash"
      argument: "{\"command\":\"find / -maxdepth 8 -iname \\\"CLAUDE.md\\\" 2>/dev/null; echo \\\"---dirs---\\\"; ls /opt/headwater-egress 2>/dev/null; echo \\\"---\\\"; ls /opt/headwater-harness 2>/dev/null\",\"description\":\"Search for CLAUDE.md and inspect headwater dirs\"}"
      result: ""
    - tool: "Bash"

      result: ""
    - tool: "Bash"
      argument: "{\"command\":\"grep -n -i \\\"derived.fold\\\\|derived total\\\\|headwater-regenerate\\\\|merge-regenerate\\\\|regenerate\\\" CLAUDE.md\",\"description\":\"Search CLAUDE.md for merge driver clues\"}"
      result: ""
    - tool: "Bash"
      argument: "{\"command\":\"cd /var/tmp/hw-1659/b/ws/L3-campaign-no-hook-p2-r86\\ngrep -rln -i \\\"derived fold\\\\|derived total\\\\|headwater-regenerate\\\\|merge-regenerate\\\" docs/ 2>/dev/null\\necho \\\"---gitattributes---\\\"\\ngrep -n \\\"merge\\\" .gitattributes | head -50\\necho \\\"---hook script---\\\"\\ncat .githooks/merge-regenerate 2>/dev/null\",\"description\":\"Search docs for merge driver and derived fold terminology\"}"
      result: ""
